                              The Director has referred to me for acknO\·Tledgement, your
                   memorandum of 4th September, 1967 on the above subject. I have since
                   discussed the matter with Superintendent/Trials, Regional Security
                   Officer, Adelaide, and Cl1ief Security Officer, l~elbourne .
                   2.        4l.-ising from t hese discussions , I now wish to advise that the
                   situation 1·egarding the reporting of "sightings" on the \~oomera Range
                   'dll be deal·t with as follows :

                               (a)     If any "sighting" should be reported by any member
                                       of the staff of Ranges Group, Woomera, this fact
                                       will, in future , be notified through P. O./Ranges
                                       and Superintendent/Trials to this Office.
                               (b)        Such reports will be checked by Senior staff in
                                       Trials Wing to ensure that tl1e level of r epo1-ting
                                       is acceptable.

                               (c)        Only reports of 11 sightings 11 which can not be
                                          othen.rise satisfactorily explained will be forwarded
                                          to the Security Branch and by tl1em to the Department
                                          of Air.
                               (d)        The Security Officer at Woomera will be advised by
                                          P.O./Ranges of any report forwarded to Superintendent/
                                          Trials but \.ri 11 take 110 further action.
                               (e)     Any report checked and passed by Trials Wing at
                                       Salisbury will be forwarded by me to the Regional
                                       Security Officer, Adelaide for on- forwarding to
                                       the Chief Security Officer and the Department of
                                       Air.

                   3.         The above procedures have been proposed with a vie,., to ensuring
                   that only reliable reports of 11 sightings 11 which have been scientifically
                   vetted wjll be fort·rarded to the Department of Air and that this Establishment
                   will be informed of all such 11 sightings 11 before they are reported to outside
                   organisations. This should prevent a:ny misunderstanding of the kind that
                   might otherv1ise arise.

     YOUR REFERENCE                                   56/4a3
                         SUBJECT                      "UNIDENTIFIED FLYING O:BJECTS" CLu:B


                                              On Mq 11th, 1966, I wrote to your predecessor about a
                                   proposal to fo:an a U.F.O. Club in Woomera.. I pointed out the
                                   dangers from the security point of view in the existence of suoh a
                                   olub in an area where there might well be "sightings" of upper
                                   atmosphere events which could not be publicised.
                                      2.         I have heard no more about the proposed club and had
                                      assumed that the proposal had died. It now appears, however, that
                                      there is in existence at Woomera some bod1' called the "Soientirio,
                                      Technical and Astronomical Research Society'" to which members
                                      report alleged U.F.O. sightings, including observations made while
                                      they a.re on duty at the Range.
                                      3.     Will you please advise me what happened after my
                                   memorandum of J4a.y" 11th 1966 was received and, in particular,
                                   whether the olub or society was fom.ed with or without official
                                   blessing. It is clear that we shall have to take some action from
                                   a security point of view, and I should like to have your views
                                   before deciding what should be done.


                                                                                        DIRECTOR

                                            Minutes of the

            Scientific. Technical and Astronomical Research Society keld K;y, 2nd, 1961.

            Those present werea        President: Mr. J . McCarthy          Apolcgies:   Mr.· J . ·Wh.i tma.rsh

            The President declared the meeting open at 1215 hours ,

            Moved Mr. Adams, seconded Mr . Patterson, that the minutes of the last meeting
            be acceptedo ~ ca.riied.

            Business Arising f'rom the Minutes:

            With reference to letter (4), the Secretary advi sed that she bad contacted Mr.
            Mathison by phone and he had apologised for not answering the letter inviting
            him to become a committee member, due to the fact that he had been on leave for
            an extended period 1 but was only too pleased to join the committee, and would
            forward written confirmation uf this before the next meeting.

            With reference to :ette~ (6), the President advised that no reply had, as yet,
            been received from NICAP.
            Discussion Meeting:

            The Discussi0n Group, scheduled for 17th April , had to be cancelled due to the
            non- availability of films . The Secretary reported that all members had been
            notified.

            Treasurerrs Report:
            On the Treasurer's behalf, the Secretary advised that an~ amendment should be
            inserted to the balance, wh6ch should :.iave read $62.•88 vice $79 . 88 as published.
            NEW BUSINESS.

            Next Discussion Evenings

            It was unanimously decided that the next Discussion Group be held on .MondaJ'",
            22nd May.. Due to the unavailability of the Theatre .Annex prior to this date,
            the delay in showing the films previously advertised is unavoidable . The Pre-
            sident advised that he would check re the availability of the pre-named films
            for this date, with NASA.

            Treasurer's Report:

            On the Treasurer! s behalf, the Secretary reported that the balance of $62. 88
            remained unchanged from March 22nd minutes .
            Librarian 1 s Report:

            Mr . Adams reported that four new magazines were available :f'.rom the Library,
            and once again expressed disappointment at the lack of interest shown by mem-
            bers in publications held by him.

            Secretary's Report:

            The Secretary reported that the May Journal had been distributed to members
            on May 1st. The June Journal would be under way in the nearfuture .    Mr. Russell
            advised the Secretary that the ~Case for the UFO ' was no longer available to the
            Society for serialisation in the Journal, so therefore, this unfortunately had
            to be dropped from publication •.
            Receipts for the Library:

            Incoming for this month were: 'The UFO Investi.gator 1 1 'Project Blue Book, Maroh
            J67•, 1 Library of Congress Legislative Asembly Services reference on UFOs ' and

            Together with this was a list of recommended reading on the subject of UFOs.
            (Letter and list will be published in the June Journal) .

            Correspondanoe 1 0UT 1 s

     appr~-x:imately 12" in diameter and appeared to ha~e a vapour trail of approximately

       far as it pur1)or ts to be 11 a conpilation" of sightings and events it is reasonably

           Since it is obvious that UFOs are spaceshi:ps " it is really not very difficult to
       go a little further and believe experiences such as are claimed by Adaoski and
       Allingham" the reade-r is assured~
          Further, "most Australian ufologists were ready to accept the (fact) • o o ~
       tha t Adamski had really oade the historic meeting with a being from outer space:., y

       ski. For example, no mention is made of the foroidable NICAP investiga tion into

                 Paul Thomas: Flyin~_§_aucers Through the Ages (translated by Gavin Gibbon)
            Few topics have the power to excite universal imagination more than that of
       Unidentified Aerial Phenonenao Whether the mass of readers is convinced about the
       existence OT flying saucers is not necessarily relevant, for even where doubt per-
       sists, interest is nore likely to be sustained .
            But what must be regarded as important is the standard of intellectual inquiry
       that is publ i shed on the subject. So ~ articles and books can be claimed as use-
       ful instruments in the pur::r.::.a.r..:; ..) 2:. ~ ..,...,_J_h; c-' ~o rs d0 little but hinder and confusec
       "Flying Saucers Through the Ages" is one of these . The contents bear little relat-
       ion to the titleo Anyone searchi ng for logical discussion will find this work in-
       cooprehensible , and for the reader acqua~~1ted with theology ( and greater part is
       critical of Biblical scholarship) it is a private viewpoint punctuated by isolated
       quotations, apparently chosen by me thod of r~dom selection.
            Writing vnder a non-de-plume, Paul hlisrak:i'. seems to have drawn inspiration from
       recent · verific at ion by science that the origin of organic material may be outer sp-
       ace . From this assu~ption a challeng e is atteripted and directed mainly against
       doctrines of Divine Revelat ion through scripture - a challenge quite legitimate i f
       done with some deg-ree of cor!!petenceo The nanne-r of attack here is by means only of
       inadequate expression of superficial observations, reGliniscent of the emotions of
       an evangelist propounding against the t heory of evolutiono Could be, that evangel-
       ists and writers of popular music like Misraki possess one comoon trait - the tend-
       ency in the modern wurld of wa.ntill6 to deny men their rights to objective critical
 •     faculty~       There are times when this art of hoodwinking is legitiaately practiced
       and undoubtedly welcorJed!' but here the reaction is one of irritation.
            At the outset, i t is extraordinary that a book bearing this title should~ within
       the total space of less than 200 pages between its covers, devote whole chapters
       in quite redundantly informing the reader that .Angelos is derived from the Greek
       ' r.iessage ' , reminding him that the end of the world has not yet taken placeo A half
       chapter goes to a fable; another two chapters to Fatima and to the Vatican respec t-
       ively o The author seems either reluctant or quite inadequa te to tell us much about
       r the c:iges ' o
            To be fair, ancient civilisations do earn raentiono Chaldea, for instance, has a
       couple of sentences worthc Rejecting historical opinions concerning mysteries sur-
       round Babylonian oatheoatical genius , he offers no opportunity to meditate the wo-
       rth of his own alternat; ve view. No oention is raade of points in secular history
       tha t one might expect to be presented ~s advantageous to his 0auseo Some of these
       could include the a1)pare:iit pre-0c-cup3tio11 in sr-ience generally, and for astronooy
       in particular, among those ancient peoples; the wonders and beginnings of Mesopot-
       a.nian Ziggurats, Egyptian Pyramids together with their associated ' light ' cults,
       sky-chariot beliefs~ and geometrics, which involved so much time and energies along
       a whol e chain of civilized cotnr.lunities of the periodo On page 16, the author ann-

           ced an intentionto "fit the pieces of a jigsaw together, to examine the facts 1 "
         ut the pieces are never produced, the facts never mentioned.
           In the li5ht of ~odern scholarship and of ser ious research, opinions cannot be
        expounded from an acadeoic vacuum. " If evidence of the visitations is more numer-
        ous than was at first thought", he says, " it is essential to alter our viewpoint".
        Despite that grammatical error contained within this sentence (the translator ' s
        fault no doubt) the truth of it remains, never theless - it only remains for some-
        one other than Mtsraki to produce the evidence . For through hin, even Biblical

        thither a ttempting nibbles at various gr ains of scriptual information - the result

                                        CONSTITUTION

                                           for

                SCIENTIFI6, TECHNICAL AND ASTRONOMICAL RESEARCH SOCIETY .

1. N.AME8     The Society vvill be known a.s the Scientific , Technical and Astronomical
              Research Society, it will be J.•:novm hereinafter as "The Society".

2.   Ail~Sg   The aims of the Society are to promote interest in the investigation of
              Aerial Phenomena on a strictly scientific basis9 to collect data on
              sightings both in Australia and overseas; in a long- term project attempt
              to analyse data collected through computer processing; subscribe to              •
              selected magazines both national and interna tional for worldwide sighting
              report oorTelation; to hold discussion groups on the scientific aspect of
              aerial phenomena; to mount a skywatch at irregular intervalsJ to be
              available to assess local sighting reports (if requested) .

3. MElvIBERSHIP:
              Membership is open to all interested per sons living and/or working in
              the area controlled.;[)y Superintendent Woomera. Any person will cease
              to be a member of the Society on leaving the area permenently.

4 . 1'1EiVIBERSHIP FEEi
      4. 1    The Society membeTship fee will be $4 per person 9 per year payable on
              joining (for shorter periods $1 per quarter) and ther eafter at the date
              of the Annual General Meeting.

      4~ 2    A General Meeting of members has the power to authori se the raising of a
              levy on members consistent with the aims of the Soci ety should the need
                 •
              arise o

      4.3     Only financial members are el igible to vote on any motion at the Anhual
              General MeetirlbJ to draw from the library any literature purchased by
              the Society for the use of me.~.1bers, and to take part in any activity
              pl anned by the Committea o

5. COMli l TTEE:
      5. 1    The Committee will consist of a President, Vice- President, Secretary,
              Treasurer, Librarian, Scientific Advisory Officer, Sighting Report
              Officer and four committeemeno

      5. 2    The Committee will have t he p ov-1er to second such member or members as
              it deems necessary to assist in the organising of particular activities .

      5.3     The Committee will have the power to appoint a member or members to
              fill vacancy or vacancie s caused by the resignation 9 posting or transfer
              from within the bounds of membership of a member of the Committee, i f it
              is deemed necessary provided tha t, should one or more vacancies occur
              t\1110 month s before the Annual General Meeting, the Committee is bound to
              make the necessary appointments~

      5. 4    Term of office :  The term of office will be one year with all committee
              members retiring each year . They will be eligible for re- election.

              Duties of the Committee g                       •

                        will be responsible for the good order and conduct of all meetings.

    6. ELECTIONS:
        6. 1   Members vvill be elected to the positions of President, Vice- President,
               Secretary or Conmi ttee~en etc . at the Annual General Meeting on receipt
               of a no~ination which, after indications of acceptance, will be seconded .

        6. 2   If more seconded nominations are received than are required to fill the
               vacancied voting will be by secret ballot .

        i.3    .Any financial member may exercise one vote only on any mo tion put to the
               meeting, and one vote only for each of the r equired number of candidates .

        6. 6   The Annual General Meeting will elect in order, a President 9 Vice- President,
               Secretary, Treasurer, Librarian, Scientific Advisory Officer, Sighting
               Report Officer and Committeemen.

    7. MEETINGS :
        7. 1   The Society ' s financial year will end on 31st July .

        7.2    The Annual General Meeting will be held within six weeks of the end of
               the Society's financial year .

        7.3    The notice of the Annual General Meeting, together with the agenda and
               balance sheet, will be posted in accordance with para 5. 5.3 of this
               Constitution.

        7. 4   Conmittee nee tings will be held once per calendar month between Annual
               General meetings 1 and may be called at any time at the discretion of the
               Chairman.

        7. 5   Ex:traordinary General Meetings will be called by the Chairman at his
               discretion, or upon receipt by the Chairman, in writing, of a petition
               to hold such a meeting, "Vvhich r equ est will set out clearly the desired
               r easons for calling the meeting and t:lU.st be signed by ten percent of the
               financial ne~bers on the register a t that time .

        7. 6   A me eting to discuss the contents of the petition will be held within
               twenty-one daJTs of the receipt of the petition. Notice of the date of
               the ~ee ting , together with the agenda, will be distributed to all
               members in accordance with para 5. 5.3 of this Constitution .
    -3 • QUORUMS :

        8.1    Goneral Meetings . A quorum for all General Meeting s will consist of
               twenty- five percent of all members on the register at that time .

        8. 2   ifuere a mee ting is postponed for lack of a quorun, notice of the
               recallEd meeti ng will be duly posted stating clearly that those members
               present at the recalled meeting will constitute a qu0rum.

        8. 3   Committee Meetings .   A quorum for all committee meetings vJill consist
               of six members who will include President or Vice-President, plus the
               Secretary or appo inted Deputy.

                            No motion duly passed at a General Meeting of the Society may be
                            rescinded within a period of twelve months unless notice of motion is
                            posted in accordance with para 5. 5. 3 of this Constitution.

          11 . HONORARIA ~
                            The Committee may decide from time to time to award honoraria to such
                            people as, in the opinion of t he Committee, have render ed exceptional
                            service to the Societyo

          12 . TERMINATION OF THE SOCIETY:

                            Prior to the termination of the Socie ty, an Ex:traordinary General
                            Mee ting will b e c alled to decide the disposal of any monies or goods
                            belonging to t he Socie ty at tha t time .

          13 . POWER OF VETO :

                   13 . 1   The Superintendent Vl/oomera or his appointed Deputy has the pov~1 er to veto
                            any decision involving activities in the Woorner a area or directly
                            concerning Woomera ar ea oper ations .

                   13 . 2   To safeguard the power of veto a copy of t he ~inute s of any meeting will
                            be sent to Superintendent Woomer a (or his appointed Deputy) within sev0n
                            days of such mae tingo

          14. SECURITY:
                            The s ecurity conditions for the oper a tion of the Soci ety within the
                            Woooera Area will be as laid down in the Conditions of Entry to
                            Restricted Areas, The Undertaking of Secrecy, and as directed by the
                            Superintendent Woomera. These must be strictly adhered to at all times .

                                         CONSTITUTION
                                            for

                        Scientific, Technical and Astronomical Research Society

                                     as at 27th July, 1966., subject to ~pproval of
                                        Superintendent Woomera.

       1. NAME:   The Society will be known as the Scientific, Technical and Astronomical
                  Research Society, it will be known hereinafter as 0 The Society".

       2. AIMS:   The aims of the Society are to promote interest in the investigation
                  of Aerial Phenomena on a strictly scientific basisJ to collect data
                  on sightings both in Australia and overseasJ in a long-term projeot
                  attempt to analyse data collected through computer processingf sub-
                  scribe to selected magazines both national and international for
                  worldwide s ighting report correlation; to hold discussion groups on
                  the scientific aspect of aerial phenomena; to mount a skywatch at
                  irregular intervalsf to be available to assess looal sighting reports
                  (it reques t ed).

       3. MEMBERSHIP:
                   Membership is open to all interested persons living and/or wor king
                   in the area contr olled by Superintendent Woomera. Any person will
                   cease to be a member of the Society on leaving the area permenently.

       4. M])IBERSHIP FEE:
           4.1     The Society member ship fee will be 84 per person, per yea:r payable
                   on joining (for shorter periods 81 par quarter) and theraafter at the
                   da te of the Annual General Meeting.

           4.2     A General Meeting of members has the power to authorise the raising
                   of a levy on members consistent with the aims of t he Society should
                   the need arise.
           4 .3    Only financial members are eligible to vote on axiy motion at the
                   Annual General Meetingf to draw from the library aJ13' literature
                   purchased by the Society for the use of members, and to take part
                   in any activity pl anned by the Cormnittee.

        5. COMMITTEE:
            5.1    The Committee will consis t of a President, Vice-President, Secretary,
                   Treasurer, Librarian, Scientific Advisory Officer, Sighting Report
                   Officer and four commi t teemen.

            5.2    The Committee wil l have t he power t o second such member or members
                   as it deems necessary to assi s t in the organising of particular
                   activities.

            5.3    The Committee will have the power to appoint a member or members to
                   fill vacancy or vacancies caused by the resignation, posting or
                   transfer from within the bounds of membership of a member of the
                   Counnittee, if it is deemed necessary provided that, should one or
                   more vacancies occur two months before the Annual General Meeting,
                   the Committee i s bound to make the necessary appointments.

            5.4    Term of Offices The term of office will be one yea:r with all
                   committee members retiring each year. They wil l be eligible for
                   re-election.

            5.5    Duties of the Committee:
                   5.5.1 President. Tb.e President will act as Chairman at all meetingsJ
                             will be responsible for the good order and conduct of all meetings.
                             He will not vote on a:ny motion except when a casting vote is
                             necessary at all General and Committee meetings.


National Archives of Australia                                   NAA: 0250, 56/3568 PART
                                                                                       _1_J
   ...   •                                2•


                     5.5.2 Vice-President. The Vice-President will assist the President,
                           and act for the President at a:ny time the latter is unable
                           personally to attend to the business of the Society.

                     5.5.3 Secreta.ry. The Secretary will prepare an agenda for all
                           meetings; record the minutes of all meeitingsf originate and sign,
                           at the committee's direction, outward correspondence; maintain a
                           register of all membersJ advise all concerned of forthcoming
                           meetings seven clear days before the meeting is called. The
                           Secretary is to have the power to second assistants to write
                           personally to other clubs or societies for information.

                     5.5.4 Treasurer. The Treasurer will receive all dues on behalf of the
                           Society; bank all monies in the Society's Account within a week of
                           receiptJ keep all necessary books of account to record the
                           Society's financial business; present a statement of income and
                           expenditure at each regular General and Committee meetingJ maintain
                           in conjunction with the Secretary a petty cash imprest of an
                           amount to be decided upon by the committee from time to time, which
                           amount will not exceed $5 without reference to a General meeting;
                           to have the power to draw cheques or withdrawal forms for
                           payment of any commitment B8I"eed by the committee.

                     5.5.5 Librarian. The Librarian will be responsible for the filing,
                            listing and safe-keeping of all journals, periodicals, magazines
                            and novels owned by the Soeietyf will ensure that when the a.bove
                            items are drawn by members that they are correctly stamped and
                            recorded in the appropriate files; that time allowed for reading
                            any of the above-mentioned literature will not exceed two weeks
                            for a novel and one week for periodioalsJ i f the time taken for
                            reading exceeds the specified period that a fine of one cent per
                            item per day be imposed for each additional day that the item
                            remains on loa.n.

                     5.5.6 Scientific Advisory Officer. The Scientific Advisory Officer will
                            be available for sighting report analysisJ present scientific
                            aspects of aerial phenomena at discussionsJ correlate data for
                            long-term project analysis attempts as in Para 2. (Aims) of this
                            Constitution and any other matter dealing with scientific aerial
                            data.

                     5.5.7 Sighting Report Officer.    The Sighting Report Officer will be
                            responsible for sighting report form distribution and collection;
                            the giving of a.ny aid required for compilation of such reports; to
                            keep a file of processed s.i ghting reportsf to collect newscuttings
                            relating to aerial pli;:anomena and to have the power to second mem-
                            bers for collection of newscuttings.

                            5.5. 7.1 Range Sighting Reports. For clearance to gather such
                                     reports the Head of the respective Section in which the
                                     sighting was reported, together with the Security officer,
                                     must be approached before any data is gathered.

                     5.5.8 Committeemen. The Committeemen will assist in all Committee
                            matters, and table views of all members as they are received.

                     5.5.9 The Committee may suspend any member from the Society for such
                            period(s) as shall be determined by the Committee, where the
                            Committee is s a tisfied that the member's mis-conduct is
                            prejudicial to the good order and conduct of the Society.

                            5.5.9.1 Before any disciplinary measure is imposed on any member,
                                     that member will be given an opportunity of appearing
                                     before the comrrdttee to answer the allegations made
                                     against him.

                    5.5.10 The Committee is empowered to rule on all questions of eligibility
                            o:f membership.


National Archives of Australia                                 NAA: 0250, 56/3568 PART 1
        ..

                       5.5.11   The Committee will be solely responsible for authorising the
                                organisation of any Sooiety function.

                       5.5.12 The Commjttee will appoint an auditor and will promulgate the
                                name of the auditor within one month of the appointment.

                       5.5.13 All accounts for payment nust be authorised for paJment by a
                                simple majority of the Society Committee. This authorisation is
                                to apply to all normal activity and subscription aooounts.
             6. ELECTIONSs
                6.1    Members will be elected to the positions of President, Vice President,
                       Secretary or Committeemen etc at the Annual General Meeting on receipt
                       of a nomination which, after indications of acceptance, will be seconded.
                6.2    If more seconded nominations are reoeived than are required to fill the
                       vacancies voting will be by secret ballot.

                6.3    Any financial member may exeroise one vote only on aXlY motion put to the
                       meeting, and one vote only for each of the required number of candidates.

                6.4    The required number of nominees who individually gain the highest number
                       of votes will be elected to the oommittee.

                6.5    Two members who have neither nominated a member, nor themsel ves been
                       nominated, will be appointed by the Chairman to ac t as scrutineers of the
                       ballot.

                6.6    The Annual General meeting will elect in order, a President, Vice President,
                       Secretary, Treasurer, Librarian, Scientific Advisory Officer, Sighting
                       Report Officer and Committeemen.
             7. MEETINGS:
                7.1    The Society's financial year will end on 31 st July.

                7.2    The Annual General Meeting will be held within six: weeks of the end of
                       the Society's financial yea:r.

                7.3    The notice of the Annual General Meeting, together with the agenda and
                       balance sheet, will be posted in accordance with para 5.5.3 of this
                       Constitution.

                7.4    Committee meetings will be held once per calendar month between Annual
                       General meetings, and may be called at any time at the discretion of the
                       Chairman.

                7.5    Extraordinary General meetings will be called by the Chairman at his
                       discretion, or upon receipt by the Chairman, in writing, of a petition
                       to hold such a meeting, >.v hich request will set out clearly the desired
                       reasons for calling the meeting and must be signed by ten percent of the
                       financial members on the register at that time.

                       A meeting to discuss the contents of the petition will be held within
                       twent.,v-one days of the receipt of the petition. Notice of the date of
                       the meeting, together with the agenda, will be distributed to all
   •
                       members in accordance with Para 5.5.3. of this Constitution•
             B. QUORUMS:
                8.1    General Meetings. A quorum for all General Keeti~s will consist of
                       twenty-five percent of all members on the register at that time.
               a.2     Where a meeting is postponed for lack of a quorum, notice of the
                       recalled meeting will be duly posted stating clearly that those members
                       present at the recalled meeting will constitute a quorum.


National Archives of Australia                                      NAA: 0250, 56/3568 PART 1
   ...   (


                       Committee Meetings• A quorum for all committee meetings will oonsist
                       of six members who will include President or Vice-President, plus the
                       Secretary of appointed Deputy.
                       Where a Committee meeting is postponed for lack of a quorum, notice of
                       the recalled meeting will be duly posted stating clearly that those members
                       present at the recalled meeting will constitute a quorum.
             9. RECEIPTS:
                       All receipts will be banked in the Society's Aooount to be determined
                       by the Committee, and all cheques and other instruments drawn will be
                       signed on behalf of the Committee by any two from the President,
                       Treasurer and Secretary.
             10. RESCINDING A MOTION:

                       No motion duly passed at a General Meeting of the Society may be
                       rescinded within a period of twelve months unless notice of motion is
                       posted in accordance with Para 5.5.3 of this Constitution.
             11 • HONORARIA:

                       The Committee may decide from time to time to award honoraria to such
                       people as, in the opinion of the Committee, have rendered exceptional
                       service to the Society.

             12. TERMINATION OF THE SOCIETY:
                      P~ h"
                        n th.a...eve11:t_gf the termination of the Society, an Extraordinary General
                       Meeting will be called to decide the disposal of any monies or goods
                       belonging t o the Society at that time.
             13. POWER OF VETOa

                13.1   The Superintendent Woomera or his appointed Deputy has the power to veto
                       any decision involving activities in the Woomera area or directly
                       concerning Woomer~ area operations.

                       To safeguard the power of veto a copy of the minutes of ~ meeting will
                       be sent to Superintendent Woomera (or his appointed Deputy) within seven
                       days of suoh meeting.
             14. SECURITY:

                       Security cond tions for the operation of the Society within the Woomera
                       area will be. as directed by Superintendent Woomera and must be strictly
                       adhered to at all times.
             15. ALTERING THIS CONSTITUTION:

                       This Constitution may only be altered at a General Meeting.

                       A motion to alter the Constitution will be carried by a two-thirds
                       majority of members present.

                       Notice of the motion to alter this Constitution will be posted in
                       accordance with Para 5.5.3 of this Constitution before suoh motion
                '
                       may be heard at a General Meeting.

Tl1e Director,
1veapons Research Establishment,
lvoomera. S .~.

 Dear Sir,
               re Unidenti:fie.d Object sighdled 1 J/6/66.

 At about midnight on the above mentioned date a group of natives were
 hunting by spotlight for kangaroos some ttV"enty miles north of the
 lvarburton Ranges Mission. They have described to me an object sighted
 by them that we felt may be of interest to you. Their description to me
 1'las that i t was some object of very large proportions, 'much larger than
 the moon~, and was travelling from a Westerly direction to the East, at
 an altitude of about ttvo eighths above the horizon . It appeared to be        ·•
 burning with a very red glo'v and wi tl1 a very long tail, tvi th large pieces
 burning and :falling away in its trail. As i t neared the Eastern horizon
 i t appeared to :fa.l l to the ground . They say that they could hear nothing.
 They described i t as being different from the 'Falling stars' with which
 t hey are familiar.
 Should there be a11y :furth·e r information that tve could supply we shall be
 happy to do so. Several of the natives concerned are relatively well
 informed on the point that there are numerous objects (man-made) orbiting
 th.e earth, and t;hey are 1iilJ.ing to give you any information on this ma tter
 mentioned above.

                                                                          FOR ENQUIRIES REFER TO


                                                                          DATE


     SURJE<;T


                  2.
                                                                          ---
                               I agree that any proposal to form an Unidentified Flying
                  Object Club in Woomera should not be supported officially.    On the
                  other hand, I doubt whether we can deny Yoomera personnel getting
                  together as a group or persons interested in such matters, and indeed
                  there would be some merit in the Club being properly formed and operat-
                  ing 'in the open ' •
                  3.          However, I feel that such an organisation could place us
                  in an embarrassing situation by reporting incidents and requesting
                  information on incidents which we cannot publicise. Even if we were
                  to excuse ourselves on the score of security, we would be admitting to
                  the existence of' classified activities at particular times and stimulate
                  further interest.
                  4.          In the circl1Dlstances, if you are approached officially on
                  the matter, you should point out that lfoomera is a prohibited area and
                  the disclosure of information relevant to the area is prohibited. In
                  the circumstances, any Club or this nature would not be able to pass
                  information to the Australian Society or any other Clubs.
                  5.          If the representations persist in the light of that advice,
                  I would need to consider whether the matter should be considered by the
                  Security Policy Committee.


                                                                     DTBECTOR
